    :276    # 6. The Valence Dichotomy
    :278    > *"He did not know whether he was Chuang Tzu who had dreamt he was a butterfly, or a butterfly dreaming that he was Chuang Tzu. Between them there must be some distinction."*
    :279    > — Chuang Tzu
    :281    ## 6.1. Splitting in the Seven Fields
    :283    **Definition 6.1.** The valence of a prime p > 3 is the number of fields ℚ(√d), for d ∈ {−3, −2, −1, 2, 3, 6, −6}, in which p splits (i.e., (d/p) = +1).
    :285    **Theorem 6.2.** Every prime p > 3 has valence exactly 3 or exactly 7.
    :287    *Proof.* The seven Legendre symbols are determined by three independent symbols (−1/p), (2/p), (3/p), each ±1. If all three are +1: all seven products are +1, giving valence 7. Otherwise: direct enumeration shows valence 3 in every case. No other valence occurs. ∎
    :289    **Corollary 6.3.** The valence dichotomy is {3, 7} = {dim 𝕋 − 4, dim 𝕋}. The gap — valences 0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 6 — is structurally forbidden.
    :291    ## 6.2. Dirichlet Characters mod 24
    :293    **Proposition 6.4.** The natural modulus of the Trivium is 24 = 2³ × 3. There are exactly 8 Dirichlet characters mod 24, corresponding to the 8 elements of (ℤ/24ℤ)*.
    :295    *Proof.* The group (ℤ/24ℤ)* has order φ(24) = 8, and by CRT, (ℤ/24ℤ)* ≅ (ℤ/8ℤ)* × (ℤ/3ℤ)* ≅ (ℤ/2)² × ℤ/2. The 8 characters classify splitting behavior completely. ∎

    :462    The Möbius Dichotomy (Theorem 7.5) states that the real line bundle E_ℝ over the critical line is non-orientable with monodromy −1. Combined with a classical symmetry of ξ, this yields:
    :464    **Theorem 8.14 (FOCUS).** On the critical line, ξ(1/2 + it) ∈ ℝ for all real t. The image of ξ collapses from ℂ (2-dimensional) to ℝ (1-dimensional).
    :466    *Proof.* The functional equation gives ξ(s) = ξ(1−s). The Schwarz reflection principle gives ξ(s̄) = ξ(s)̄. At s = 1/2 + it: 1 − s = 1/2 − it = s̄. Therefore ξ(s) = ξ(1−s) = ξ(s̄) = ξ(s)̄, so ξ(s) equals its own conjugate, hence ξ(s) ∈ ℝ. [Standard; Riemann 1859, Edwards 1974.] ∎
    :468    *Remark 8.15.* The Möbius twist is the topological encoding of the symmetry that produces FOCUS. The monodromy T² = −I on the real fiber corresponds to sign reversal — the geometric content of a real-valued function whose zeros are codimension-1 sign changes. The analytic thread (functional equation
    :470    > 🟢 *This is the moment the paper turns. Everything built so far — the identity subspace, the vector, the spectrum, the spin, the Möbius twist, the trefoil — was structure.     :472    ## 8.6. The Codimension Dichotomy
    :474    FOCUS creates an asymmetry between the critical line and the rest of the strip.
    :476    **Proposition 8.16 (Codimension Dichotomy).** Zeros of ξ on the critical line have codimension 1. Zeros of ξ off the critical line have codimension 2.
    :478    *Proof.* On the critical line, FOCUS gives ξ(1/2 + it) ∈ ℝ. A zero requires Re(ξ) = 0, one real condition on one real variable t. This is codimension 1. The Intermediate Value Theorem produces such zeros generically: sign changes of continuous real functions. Off the critical line, ξ(σ + it) ∈ ℂ wit
    :480    *Remark 8.17.* Codimension-1 conditions generically have solutions; codimension-2 conditions generically do not. The distinction is not between probable and improbable but between structurally expected and structurally exceptional.
    :482    The Codimension Dichotomy explains *why* zeros exist on the critical line (the FOCUS mechanism produces them via IVT) and *what would be required* for off-line zeros (a codimension-2 coincidence with a specific cause). The topological results of §§7–8.3 sharpen this: Holonomy Concentration (Theorem 
    :484    > 🟢 *On the critical line, finding a zero requires one thing: the real-valued function changes sign. One condition, one variable. Off the critical line, finding a zero requires two things to happen at the same height: the real part vanishes AND the imaginary part vanishes. Two conditions, one variab
    :486    ## 8.7. Level Set Geometry
    :488    The codimension-2 condition for off-line zeros has a geometric interpretation in terms of level curves. We develop this geometry in detail, proving several structural results that establish persistent family separation from the critical line through the full strip.
    :490    **Definition 8.18.** Write ξ(s) = u(σ,t) + iv(σ,t) with u = Re(ξ), v = Im(ξ). Define two families of curves in the critical strip: Family R = {(σ,t) : u(σ,t) = 0} and Family I = {(σ,t) : v(σ,t) = 0}. A zero of ξ at s₀ is a point where both families pass through s₀.
    :492    On the critical line, FOCUS gives v(1/2, t) = 0 for all t, so the entire critical line belongs to Family I. The zeros γ₁ < γ₂ < γ₃ < ⋯ of ξ on the critical line (where u(1/2, γₖ) = 0) are the points where Family R crosses. Between consecutive zeros, u(1/2, t) is a continuous real function that does 
    :494    **Proposition 8.19 (Interleaving).** On the critical line, zeros and extrema of u(1/2, t) = ξ_ℝ(t) strictly alternate: γ₁ < t*₁ < γ₂ < t*₂ < γ₃ < ⋯ .
    :496    *Proof.* Between consecutive zeros γₖ and γₖ₊₁, the function ξ_ℝ(t) is continuous, nonvanishing, and changes sign. By the extreme value theorem on [γₖ, γₖ₊₁], it attains an extremum at some interior point t*ₖ. Between consecutive extrema t*ₖ and t*ₖ₊₁, ξ_ℝ must change sign (it has opposite signs at 
    :498    **Proposition 8.20 (Family Separation on the Critical Line).** On the critical line, Family R passes through heights {γₖ} and Family I passes through heights {t*ₖ}. By Interleaving (Proposition 8.19), these height sets are disjoint and alternating. Therefore, at σ = 1/2, no point of Family R coincid
    :500    *Proof.* Immediate from Proposition 8.19 and the fact that zeros and extrema of ξ_ℝ are distinct (ξ_ℝ(γₖ) = 0 while ξ_ℝ(t*ₖ) ≠ 0 at an extremum). ∎
    :502    ### Symmetry of the components
    :504    **Proposition 8.24 (Symmetry Identities).** For all (σ, t) in the critical strip: u(σ, t) = u(1−σ, t) and v(σ, t) = −v(1−σ, t). That is, u is symmetric and v is anti-symmetric about σ = 1/2.
    :506    *Proof.* The functional equation gives ξ(s) = ξ(1−s). The Schwarz reflection principle gives ξ(s̄) = ξ(s)̄. From these: u(σ,t) = Re[ξ(σ+it)] = Re[ξ((1−σ)+it)] = u(1−σ,t).     :508    *Remark 8.24a.* The symmetry u(σ,t) = u(1−σ,t) implies ∂u/∂σ(1/2,t) = 0 for all t: the critical line is a locus of σ-critical points of u. The anti-symmetry v(σ,t) = −v(1−σ,t) confirms that v vanishes identically on σ = 1/2 (FOCUS) and is odd in the variable δ = σ − 1/2.
    :510    **Proposition 8.25 (Cauchy–Riemann Expansion).** Write δ = σ − 1/2. Then: (i) v(1/2 + δ, t) = −δ · ξ′_ℝ(t) + O(δ³), where ξ′_ℝ(t) = du/dt(1/2, t). (ii) u(1/2 + δ, t) = ξ_ℝ(t) + (δ²/2)(−ξ″_ℝ(t)) + O(δ⁴), where ξ″_ℝ(t) = d²u/dt²(1/2, t).
    :512    *Proof.* Part (i): By anti-symmetry v(1/2+δ,t) = −v(1/2−δ,t), so v is odd in δ; its Taylor expansion contains only odd powers. The first-order coefficient is ∂v/∂σ(1/2,t) = −∂u/∂t(1/2,t) = −ξ′_ℝ(t) by Cauchy–Riemann. Part (ii): By symmetry u(1/2+δ,t) = u(1/2−δ,t), so u is even in δ; its expansion co
    :514    ### The Local Repulsion Theorem

    :520    **Proposition 8.27 (Orthogonal Crossing).** At each simple zero ρₖ = 1/2 + iγₖ, the derivative ξ′(ρₖ) is purely imaginary. Consequently, Family R and Family I cross at right angles at each zero.
    :522    *Proof.* Since ξ(1/2+it) ∈ ℝ for all t (FOCUS), the function t ↦ ξ(1/2+it) is real-valued.     :524    ### The σ-Convexity of |ξ|²

    :528    **Proposition 8.28 (σ-Convexity).** Define C(t) = (ξ′_ℝ(t))² − ξ_ℝ(t) · ξ″_ℝ(t). Then ∂²|ξ|²/∂σ² evaluated at σ = 1/2 equals 2C(t). Moreover, C(γₖ) > 0 at every simple zero and C(t*ₖ) > 0 at every extremum.
    :530    *Proof.* At σ = 1/2: v = 0 and ∂u/∂σ = 0 (Remark 8.24a). Differentiating |ξ|² = u² + v² twice in σ: ∂²|ξ|²/∂σ² = 2(uσ² + u·uσσ + vσ² + v·vσσ).     :532    *Remark 8.28a.* The σ-convexity C(t) > 0 means the critical line is a local minimum of |ξ|² in the σ-direction at height t.     :534    ### Neighborhood Nonvanishing
    :536    The preceding results are perturbative: they describe behavior to leading order in δ. The following theorem is exact.
    :538    **Theorem 8.29 (Neighborhood Nonvanishing).** Let γₖ be a simple zero of ξ_ℝ. The R-curve through γₖ is the locus {(σ, t_R(σ)) : u(σ, t_R(σ)) = 0} with t_R(1/2) = γₖ.     :540    *Proof.* (a) Vₖ is real-analytic in σ: the function ξ is entire, so u and v are real-analytic; the R-curve t_R(σ) is analytic by the implicit function theorem (since ∂u/∂t(1/2, γₖ) = ξ′_ℝ(γₖ) ≠ 0 at a simple zero); composition of analytic functions is analytic. (b) Vₖ(1/2) = v(1/2, γₖ) = 0 by FOCUS.

    :629    *Proof.* In the classical zero-free region, ξ(s) ≠ 0 for all s (de la Vallée-Poussin). On the R-curve, u = 0, so ξ = iv with v = Vₖ. Since ξ ≠ 0, we have Vₖ ≠ 0. ∎
    :631    *Remark 8.36a.* Combining Theorem 8.29 and Proposition 8.36: Vₖ is nonvanishing near the critical line (σ close to 1/2) and near the strip boundary (σ close to 1). The dangerous region for a potential fold is bounded on both sides: (1/2 + εₖ, 1 − c/log(|γₖ| + 2)).
    :633    ### What the fold requires
    :635    At a fold point on the R-curve, u_t = 0. Since ξ′ = u_σ − iu_t, this means ξ′ ∈ ℝ: the derivative of ξ is purely real.     :647    **(iv) Integration of the Monotonicity Formula.** The formula V′ₖ = −|ξ′|²/u_t may admit integration using known growth bounds on ξ′ (e.g., the Lindelöf hypothesis or unconditional subconvexity bounds), yielding explicit lower bounds on |Vₖ(σ)| that prevent return to zero.

    :759    # Glossary of Terms and Symbols
    :761    **{2,3}-smooth** — An integer all of whose prime factors lie in {2, 3}. The set {1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 9, 12, ...}. Størmer's theorem limits consecutive smooth pairs.
    :763    **Alexander polynomial** — For the trefoil T(2,3): Δₜ(t) = t² − t + 1 = Φ₆(t), the 6th cyclotomic polynomial. Acts as complex conjugation on the quarter-twist group ℤ/4.
    :765    **Aperture** — The fraction of spectral weight in the nonzero eigenspace: α = 1/7. Determines the Shannon entropy H ≈ 0.592 bits of the landscape layer.
    :767    **B₃ (braid group)** — The group ⟨a, b | a² = b³⟩. Simultaneously: braid group on 3 strands, knot group of the trefoil T(2,3), universal central extension of PSL₂(ℤ).
    :769    **Bright subspace** — The 1-dimensional eigenspace of M for eigenvalue λ = 12. The direction of v in ℂ⁷.
    :771    **Centered coordinate** — w = s − 1/2. Places the exponential balance point at the origin, with the additive identity at w = −1/2 and the multiplicative identity at w = +1/2.
    :773    **Codimension Dichotomy** — Proposition 8.16: zeros on the critical line are codimension 1 (one real condition); zeros off it are codimension 2 (two independent conditions). Codimension 1 is generic; codimension 2 is exceptional.
    :775    **Convexity midpoint** — The value σ = 3/4, where the Phragmén–Lindelöf bound has expended half its analytic budget. An analytic barrier, not topological.
    :777    **Covering tower** — B₃ → SL₂(ℤ) → PSL₂(ℤ), with kernels 2ℤ and {±I}. Each level carries successively more covering data.
    :779    **Critical line** — The line Re(s) = 1/2 in the complex plane; equivalently, w = 0 in centered coordinates. The axis of the functional equation and locus of the Trivium.
    :781    **Critical strip** — The region 0 < Re(s) < 1 in the complex plane, containing all nontrivial zeros of ζ.
    :783    **Dark subspace** — The 6-dimensional kernel of M. Decomposes into real redistribution (2 dim), imaginary redistribution (2 dim), and cross-sector (2 dim) components.
    :785    **E_ℂ** — The complex line bundle over the critical line with structure group ℤ/4. Orientable.
    :787    **E_ℝ** — The real line sub-bundle of E_ℂ. Non-orientable (Möbius band) due to monodromy T² = −1.
    :789    **Exponential balance point** — The value σ₀ = 1/2 satisfying n^{−σ} = n^{−(1−σ)} for all n. The exponential *balance identity* — the "third identity element" in the sense of the **locus** where all three operations' identities coincide (§4 caveat), not a literal fourth identity of one operation.
    :791    **Family I** — The level set {(σ,t) : Im(ξ(σ+it)) = 0}. Contains the entire critical line (by FOCUS) plus curves through extrema heights. See §8.7.
    :793    **Family R** — The level set {(σ,t) : Re(ξ(σ+it)) = 0}. Passes through zeros of ξ on the critical line. See §8.7.
    :795    **FOCUS** — The collapse of ξ's image from ℂ to ℝ on the critical line: ξ(1/2 + it) ∈ ℝ for all t. The analytic content of the Möbius twist (Theorem 8.14).
    :797    **Fold Criterion** — Corollary 8.31: Vₖ can vanish off the critical line only if the R-curve acquires a vertical tangent (u_t = 0). RH is equivalent to: no R-curve folds in the critical strip. Verified for 1.24 × 10¹³ zeros.
    :799    **Functional equation** — The identity ξ(s) = ξ(1−s), reflecting the completed zeta function about the critical line. Its axis is the Trivium; its topological content is the Möbius twist.
    :801    **Holonomy concentration** — The Möbius monodromy is supported at σ = 1/2 only. The holonomy radius is zero (Theorem 8.5).
    :803    **Interleaving** — Proposition 8.19: zeros and extrema of ξ_ℝ(t) on the critical line strictly alternate. Ensures Family R and Family I heights are disjoint.
    :805    **Local Repulsion** — Theorem 8.26: |v| grows linearly at zero heights and |u| grows quadratically at extremum heights as σ departs 1/2. A consequence of Cauchy–Riemann coupling.
    :807    **M (Trivium matrix)** — The rank-1 Hermitian matrix M = vv† ∈ Mat₇×₇(ℂ). Spectrum: {0⁶, 12}.
    :809    **Metaplectic cocycle** — The phase ζ = e^{iπ/4} in the Weil representation. Satisfies ζ⁴ = −1, giving 𝐖⁴ = −Id.
    :811    **Metaplectic group Mp₂(ℝ)** — The unique connected double cover of SL₂(ℝ). Consists of pairs (g, ε) where ε(τ)² = cτ + d. Not a matrix group.
    :813    **Möbius Dichotomy** — Theorem 7.5: E_ℂ is orientable, E_ℝ is non-orientable (Möbius). The geometric content of the metaplectic cover.
    :815    **Monotonicity Formula** — Theorem 8.30: V′ₖ(σ) = −|ξ′|²/u_t along the R-curve. Gives a closed-form expression controlling family separation across the entire strip. Vₖ is strictly monotone wherever u_t maintains sign.
    :817    **Neighborhood Nonvanishing** — Theorem 8.29: Vₖ(σ) = v(σ, t_R(σ)) has an isolated simple zero at σ = 1/2. The families cannot meet in any neighborhood of the critical line.
    :819    **Orthogonal Crossing** — Proposition 8.27: at each simple zero, ξ′ is purely imaginary, so Family R and Family I cross at right angles. Gives each zero a rigid local coordinate grid.
    :821    **PSL₂(ℤ)** — The modular group, isomorphic to ℤ/2 ∗ ℤ/3. Governs the local algebraic structure at the Trivium.
    :823    **Quarter-twist** — The map T: v ↦ iv. Generates ℤ/4 acting on v, with T² = −I and T⁴ = I. The 720° return is the spin-1/2 signature.
    :825    **R-curve** — A branch of Family R = {Re(ξ) = 0} passing through a zero γₖ of ξ_ℝ on the critical line. Parameterized by σ, with t_R(σ) giving the height at each σ-value. Its fold behavior governs the Riemann Hypothesis (Corollary 8.31).
    :827    **Signature bit** — The hidden ℤ/2 = ker(projectivization). Detects the unit −1 ∈ ℤ via the character (−1/·). Orthogonal to the prime-derived structure. Fourth information layer.
    :829    **Simple zero** — A zero ρ of ξ with ξ′(ρ) ≠ 0. Equivalent to the zero having multiplicity 1. All known nontrivial zeros are simple.
    :831    **𝕋 (Trivium)** — The identity subspace of arithmetic, defined as the locus w = 0 in the centered coordinate w = s − 1/2. The unique point where additive, multiplicative, and exponential identity behaviors simultaneously hold.
    :833    **Trefoil T(2,3)** — The (2,3) torus knot. Its wrapping numbers are the Trivium's generating primes. Alexander polynomial Φ₆(t) implements the functional equation on the phase.
    :835    **Trivium vector v** — The canonical vector (i√3, i√2, i, 0, 1, √2, √3) ∈ ℂ⁷. Norm 12, argument π/4, spin-1/2 structure.
    :837    **Valence** — For a prime p > 3: the number of fields ℚ(√d), d ∈ {−3,−2,−1,2,3,6,−6}, in which p splits. Always 3 or 7 (Theorem 6.2).
    :839    **Winding number** — The number of times ξ(s) winds around the origin as s traverses a closed contour. Equals 1 around each simple zero (argument principle).
    :841    **Zero-Free Nonvanishing** — Proposition 8.36: Vₖ(σ) ≠ 0 in the classical zero-free region σ > 1 − c/log(|γₖ| + 2). Combined with Theorem 8.29, bounds the dangerous region for potential folds from both sides.
    :843    **σ-Convexity** — Proposition 8.28: C(t) = (ξ′_ℝ)² − ξ_ℝ·ξ″_ℝ > 0, meaning the critical line is a local minimum of |ξ|² in the σ-direction. Proved at all zeros and extrema.
    :845    ---

    :27     ## 1. The Seven Trivium Quadratic Fields
    :29     The Trivium operates on the seven non-identity elements of $(\mathbb{Z}/2)^3$, identified with the squarefree products of $\{-1, 2, 3\} \subset \mathbb{Q}$. Each squarefree product $d$ yields a distinct quadratic field $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})$. The seven discriminants and class numbers:
    :31     | $d$ | Field | Hamming weight | Discriminant $D$ | $h(D)$ |
    :32     |:---:|:---|:---:|:---:|:---:|
    :33     | $-1$ | $\mathbb{Q}(i)$ | 1 | $-4$ | **1** |
    :34     | $2$ | $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{2})$ | 1 | $8$ | **1** |
    :35     | $3$ | $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{3})$ | 1 | $12$ | **1** |
    :36     | $-2$ | $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-2})$ | 2 | $-8$ | **1** |
    :37     | $-3$ | $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-3})$ | 2 | $-3$ | **1** |
    :38     | $6$ | $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{6})$ | 2 | $24$ | **1** |
    :39     | $\mathbf{-6}$ | $\mathbf{\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-6})}$ | **3** | $\mathbf{-24}$ | **2** |
    :41     Six fields are principal ideal domains; one is not. Class numbers computable in PARI/GP: `quadclassunit(-24).no = 2`.

    :45     ## 2. The Three Coincidences
    :47     $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-6})$ is distinguished from the other six Trivium fields by three a priori independent properties:
    :49     **(C1) Cube combinatorics.** In the $(\mathbb{Z}/2)^3$ representation, $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-6})$ corresponds to the vector $(1, 1, 1)$ — the unique vertex of Hamming weight 3. The other six vertices form complementary triples of size 3 each, exhibiting the $(3, 3, 1)$ Hamming decomposition.
    :51     **(C2) Class field theory.** $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-6})$ is the unique non-principal Trivium field. Its class group is $\mathbb{Z}/2$, generated (for example) by the prime ideal above 2: $(2, \sqrt{-6})$. The squarefree product $6 = 2 \cdot 3$ carries both prime divisors simultaneously ramified.
    :53     **(C3) Discriminant scaling.** The discriminant of $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-6})$ is $-24$. The absolute value 24 is the level-3 floor of the $\{2, 3\}$-doubling tower ($6 \to 12 \to 24$) and the natural modulus of Dirichlet characters mod 24, where $(\mathbb{Z}/24)^*$ has the $(\mathbb{Z}/2)^3$ structure 
    :55     ---
    :57     ## 3. Why the Coincidences Are Not Coincidences
    :59     The three distinctions are tied together by the *full ramification* of both primes $\{2, 3\}$ in $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-6})$:
    :61     - $\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-6})$ is the splitting field of $x^2 + 6$ over $\mathbb{Q}$; $6$ has unique factorization $2 \cdot 3$ over $\mathbb{Z}$.
    :62     - The discriminant $D = -24$ carries both ramified primes 2 and 3 (since $-6$ is squarefree and $\equiv 2 \pmod 4$, the discriminant doubles to $-24$).
    :63     - The class number $h(-24) = 2$ follows from full $\{2, 3\}$-ramification: neither ramified prime ideal is principal, and the non-trivial class lifts this obstruction.
    :64     - In the $(\mathbb{Z}/2)^3$ representation over $\{-1, 2, 3\}$, full ramification of $\{2, 3\}$ translates to nonzero coordinates in both the 2-direction and the 3-direction, combined with the $-1$ sign producing the all-ones vector $(1, 1, 1)$ — the diagonal.
    :66     The three coincidences are three views of the same fact: **$\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-6})$ is the only Trivium field where both $\{2, 3\}$ primes simultaneously fail to be principal.** The $(1, 1, 1)$ Hamming coordinate, the class number 2, and the discriminant magnitude 24 are three projections of this sin
    :68     (Kinship: this three-way Hamming-weight ↔ class-number ↔ discriminant-24 identification is one face of the substrate catalogued in `A_METHODOLOGY` §VI.3.2, *The $(\mathbb{Z}/2)^3$ six presentations*.)

### THE_DOCUMENT_CLASS_TAXONOMY.md: the tier definitions and (R19)`s KC -- phase1.5/method/THE_DOCUMENT_CLASS_TAXONOMY.md @ a79215a4 (8 lines cited, of 70)
    :14     **Tier K — Keystone-certified.** A document whose load-bearing claims are backed by a **machine-checked kernel terminal at a pin**, Gate-1-graded (DERIVES, or INTERFACES-with-a-named-premise). *Obligation:* every such claim states its **grade · terminal · pin**, and the axiom profile is written in full ("the standard three axioms {propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound}", or cleaner). *Citation rule:* **may be cited as certification** — the terminal, at its grade, is the warrant.
    :16     **Tier C — Cluster-synthesis.** A document that **organizes** certified results into a cross-system picture, panel, map, or interpretation — asserting *relationships* not individually certified. *Obligation:* state the invariant it organizes on; flag any tuple/value that is a **coordinate, not an invariant**; carry the Gate-1b discipline (classification × certification is the invariant, not a shared tuple). *Citation rule:* **cite for orientation and organization — NEVER as certification of the relationships it draws.** This is the taxonomy's load-bearing rule.
    :18     **Tier N — Notes / exploratory.** Consults, seeds, reports, drafts, dry-runs. *Obligation:* mark the exploratory grade on its face; no promotion without a status call. *Citation rule:* **reference-only** — not citable as established; a Tier-N claim is a lead, not a result. *Admission (the internal-until-fruit law, 2026-07-28):* **Tier N is the floor for *fruit*, not the parking lot for conversation** — a document is admitted only once its exploration has borne fruit (a conclusion informed, a discourse advanced, a decision enabled). Ongoing banter and speculative design stay **loom-resident** (ledger lines) until then; documents are earned, even at the floor.
    :20     **Tier E — Filing-facing.** A document written for counsel or a patent examiner rather than for a referee: invention disclosures, per-filing packages and claim-support charts, prior-art and enablement material, and the engineering notes held with them. *Obligation:* mark the attorney-facing grade on its face; carry the **sequencing law** (provisioning precedes publishing and partnering) and, wherever it reconstructs claim content, the **executor-cannot-read-the-filed-claims** caveat; every technical claim still states its **grade · terminal · pin**, exactly as Tier K requires — Tier E relaxes the audience, never the certificate. *Citation rule:* **never cited in a reader-facing paper** — Tie
    :57     **Tier KC — the finished keystone.** A document that **synthesizes a subject cluster** *and* **carries the verification apparatus**. It is a conjunction of what Tier K and Tier C each do alone, and it is named because the corpus writes such documents and had no word for them.
    :59     *Obligation.* **Every load-bearing claim is stated clearly in the body** and **carried by a row in a correspondence table placed after the front matter, where a reader meets it** — not appended where only a returning reader will find it. **Each row names what backs it in the front door’s own vocabulary** — `kernel-verified`, `theorem-supported`, `argument-supported`, `computationally-verified`, `synthesis-suggested`, `milestone-open`, `statement-grade` — and **a claim whose backing is not machine-checked says so in the row**. Plus **glossary, bibliography, and other tables as the document needs them**: the furniture is part of the class, because a finished keystone is a document a stranger c
    :61     *Citation rule, written so a reader who has never seen this taxonomy can obey it:* **A SYSTEMATIC SYNTHESIS WITH A VERIFICATION CONCORDANCE — CITE THE SYNTHESIS FOR ORIENTATION, CITE EACH CONCORDANCE ROW AT ITS STATED GRADE.**
    :63     > ### **THE GUARD, WRITTEN INTO THE CLASS AND NOT BESIDE IT: THIS CLASS CONFERS NO CITATION LICENSE THE TWO TIERS DO NOT ALREADY CONFER.** *It names a **conjunction** and a **furniture obligation**. Nothing in it permits a synthesis conclusion to be cited as certification. A row at `kernel-verified` is cited as Tier K allows; a synthesis relationship is cited as Tier C allows — **for orientation, never as certification of the relationships it draws**. The conjunction adds a table and a discipline; it adds no warrant.*
